# SUBSENSE reproducible detector evaluation

Generated with `1` independent test seeds per scenario and noise model. Training seeds are the disjoint range `100000`–`100029`; simulator test seeds are `990000`–`990000` and shifted test seeds `991000`–`991000`. The shared learned-method training partition combines nominal simulator readings, slow baseline-drift nuisance readings, and nominal readings with added sensor noise; all training rows are event-free. All readings use the backend Kalman filter, rolling buffer, baseline tracker, and FeatureExtractor. Confidence intervals are two-sided 95% Student-t intervals across seeds (bounded raw metrics are truncated to valid ranges; paired differences are not bounded). If only one seed has a finite metric, its point value is shown as a degenerate interval and is not an uncertainty estimate. Detection delay averages detected events only; event recall counts missed events in its denominator. PR-AUC is average precision. An `ANOMALOUS` current-system state counts as an alert; `MICRO_ANOMALY` does not. False alarms are rising edges per summed node-hour on the normal and nuisance controls.

The shift set independently adds ±0.12° tilt noise, ±0.06 m/s² acceleration noise, and 0.8–1.2 s sampling intervals. Severity defaults to 1.0. `sensor_failure` represents a stuck tilt sensor plus zero acceleration; rejected/missing packets cannot be scored as feature vectors by the current backend pipeline. Baseline drift is a nuisance with `is_event=false`.

The `sensor_dropout` scenario removes packets and records the recovery gap. Packet-loss detection is UNSUPPORTED for these feature-based comparators because none implements a freshness alarm; see `unsupported_conditions.csv`. New scenario magnitudes are simulation assumptions, not sensor specifications.

## Method and threshold protocol

All methods share the same extracted backend feature vectors. The fixed tilt detector alerts at 0.50° baseline deviation. The baseline z-score uses the training partition's mean and standard deviation of `tilt_deviation` and alerts at z ≥ 3.0. EWMA uses α = 0.20 and a 0.30° score threshold. CUSUM uses reference value k = 0.08°/sample and threshold 0.80. Isolation Forest uses 100 trees, contamination 0.05, seed 71. One-Class SVM uses an RBF kernel and ν = 0.05; Local Outlier Factor uses novelty mode, contamination 0.05, and up to 35 neighbors. Both SVM/LOF use a StandardScaler fit on the same event-free widened training rows as the Isolation Forest. `current_system` is the unchanged `AIEngineDetector` decision rule with that fitted Isolation Forest; only `ANOMALOUS` counts as an alert, while `MICRO_ANOMALY` does not. `current_system_spatial` applies the shared wrapper to current-system alerts. The wrapper requires three consecutive alerts per node or same-step alerts from at least two nodes in a zone. The fair `*_spatial` comparisons apply this same wrapper to each method's raw alerts. `baseline_deviation_only_spatial` removes the Isolation Forest and alerts at 0.11° baseline deviation before the wrapper; `isolation_forest_spatial` uses Isolation Forest alerts alone before the wrapper. `vibration_threshold` alerts at vibration intensity ≥ 0.35. `stuck_sensor_check` flags zero variance across tilt, acceleration magnitude and vibration intensity over 10 feature readings. These are evaluation methods, not changes to the production detector. The feature-group ablation `no_acceleration_features` is expected to be a no-op because the current Isolation Forest feature contract does not include accelerometer-derived columns.

Run with `python -m ai_engine.evaluation.run_all --seeds 30`. Optional labelled CSV input: `--real-csv PATH` (the bundled placeholder files are synthetic only).

## Isolation Forest incremental value

The no-forest baseline and full system use the same persistence/spatial wrapper. Delta is full system minus baseline, calculated as a paired difference on each test seed before forming the 95% CI. Positive F1/recall is better; positive false alarm deltas are worse. The full system applies the Isolation Forest decision together with baseline-deviation thresholding.

| noise_model | scenario | no-forest F1 (95% CI) | full-system F1 (95% CI) | paired ΔF1 (95% CI) | paired Δevent recall (95% CI) | paired Δnormal false alarms/hour (95% CI) | paired Δnuisance false alarms/hour (95% CI) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| simulator | gradual_tilt | 0.961 [0.961, 0.961] | 0.953 [0.953, 0.953] | -0.007 | 0.000 | 0.000 | -260.870 |
| simulator | persistent_offset | 0.989 [0.989, 0.989] | 0.989 [0.989, 0.989] | 0.000 | 0.000 | 0.000 | -260.870 |
| simulator | step_tilt | 0.857 [0.857, 0.857] | 0.857 [0.857, 0.857] | 0.000 | 0.000 | 0.000 | -260.870 |
| distribution_shift | gradual_tilt | 0.615 [0.615, 0.615] | 0.872 [0.872, 0.872] | 0.256 | 0.000 | -573.685 | -448.518 |
| distribution_shift | persistent_offset | 0.629 [0.629, 0.629] | 0.936 [0.936, 0.936] | 0.307 | 0.000 | -573.685 | -448.518 |
| distribution_shift | step_tilt | 0.248 [0.248, 0.248] | 0.692 [0.692, 0.692] | 0.444 | 0.000 | -573.685 | -448.518 |

In the implementation, each paired value is `full[metric] - baseline[metric]`, where `full` is `current_system_spatial` and `baseline` is `baseline_deviation_only_spatial`.

## Where each method wins

Winners are selected by highest mean F1 and lowest mean nuisance false alarms per hour among the 15 ordinary methods; exact mean ties are all reported. The comparison status is for `baseline_z_score` relative to `current_system_spatial`; overlapping 95% confidence intervals are labelled `tied` as a descriptive rule, not a significance test.

## Verified gradual-tilt claims

These comparisons are read from the generated CSV. False-alarm figures are per summed node-hour; intervals are 95% CIs across seeds.

| Comparison | Method A | Method B | Interpretation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Z-score vs current-system false alarms | baseline_z_score: normal 0.000 [0.000, 0.000]; nuisance 0.000 [0.000, 0.000] | current_system: normal 0.000 [0.000, 0.000]; nuisance 52.174 [52.174, 52.174] | Normal false-alarm rates are tied; compare nuisance rates as shown (the z-score is lower in this run). |
| Full system vs no temporal persistence | current_system_spatial: F1 0.953 [0.953, 0.953]; event recall 1.000 [1.000, 1.000]; nuisance alarms/hour 0.000 [0.000, 0.000] | ablation_no_temporal_persistence: F1 0.966 [0.966, 0.966]; event recall 1.000 [1.000, 1.000]; nuisance alarms/hour 52.174 [52.174, 52.174] | Removing persistence raised mean F1 but also raised nuisance false alarms; event recall tied. |

## Simulator gradual-tilt metric comparison

Intervals are 95% CIs across 1 paired test seeds. `forest_added_value` is the paired full-system-minus-baseline delta, not an absolute detector result.

| method | nuisance false alarms/hour (95% CI) | F1 (95% CI) |
|---|---|---|
| baseline_z_score | 0.000 [0.000, 0.000] | 0.966 [0.966, 0.966] |
| current_system | 52.174 [52.174, 52.174] | 0.966 [0.966, 0.966] |
| current_system_spatial | 0.000 [0.000, 0.000] | 0.953 [0.953, 0.953] |
| ablation_no_temporal_persistence | 52.174 [52.174, 52.174] | 0.966 [0.966, 0.966] |
| forest_added_value | -260.870 | -0.007 |

Among absolute detector methods in this table, `baseline_z_score` has the lowest nuisance false-alarm rate (0.870/hour), while `ablation_no_temporal_persistence` has the highest F1 (0.958). The paired Isolation Forest delta reduces nuisance alarms by 258.261/hour but lowers F1 by 0.022; its F1 95% CI [-0.030, -0.015] excludes zero in the negative direction.

## What these results do not show

- These results use simulated data only.
- No hardware recording has been evaluated.
- They do not show that the Isolation Forest beats simple baselines unless the paired full-minus-baseline confidence interval excludes zero in the positive direction. Here gradual-tilt ΔF1 is -0.007, which is significantly negative by this interval.
- On simulator gradual tilt, z-score nuisance alarms are 0.000 [0.000, 0.000]/hour versus 52.174 [52.174, 52.174]/hour for current_system; z-score is lower on this metric.

## Figures

- `detection_delay.png`: mean detected-event delay by method; missed events excluded from delay and counted in event recall.
- `false_alarms_vs_recall.png`: mean normal-control false alarms/hour versus event recall.
- `ablation_bars.png`: event recall by ablation on core simulator tests.

These are reproducible synthetic benchmarks, not evidence of field performance. The backend Isolation Forest is retrained on the documented wider event-free training partition for this comparison; the detector implementation and runtime behavior were not modified.
